ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u13	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present to the High House a draft law amending the Act on VAT on goods and services and certain other laws contained in print No 1114. The amendments introduced by this law aim to implement two Directives: 2017/2455 and 2019/1995, i.e. the so-called. EU e-commerce package. The aim of the EU package is to seal VAT on Internet commerce and imports so-called. small consignments to the European Union. This is a project to stop the inflow of non-taxed parcels from third countries to European Union countries. Polish and EU companies are losing their jobs. Our entrepreneurs are not able to compete with companies from the Far East who sell the same goods as Polish entrepreneurs, but cheaper about VAT, which is often 1/4 of the price. The solutions proposed in the Act will not only lead to the sealing of the system, but will also facilitate the operation of honest companies, those that operate on the e-commerce market and are registered in the European Union. How exactly? They will reduce formalities and reduce costs associated with their payment of VAT in other countries. Thanks to the new procedures, traders trading on the Internet via sales platforms will not have to register separately for VAT purposes. Nor will they have to account for themselves in each country to which they send their goods separately. Instead, they will settle VAT using a single window in one place in their country of residence. And the country in which they operate will share their tax with the country to which the shipment goes. What will be the result? The increase in VAT budget revenue, the projected budgetary impact still this year, is almost PLN 0.5 billion, and in the following years it will amount to around 1 000 200 000. PLN per year. In presenting the above, I kindly ask the High House to adopt the draft law as proposed by the government. Thank you. [[The meeting is chaired by Deputy Marshal of the Sejm Włodzimierz Czarzasty]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u15	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I have the pleasure of presenting, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, a position on the draft law amending the Law on income tax on goods and services and on certain other laws in print No 1114. The need to amend the Act of 11 March 2004 on the tax on goods and services is due to the obligation to implement EU legislation on the so-called 'Union law' in the Polish legal order. e-commerce VAT package. The Member States of the European Union, including Poland, are obliged to implement in national tax legislation the solutions provided for in the e-commerce package aimed at overcoming barriers, including VAT sealing in cross-border e-commerce between traders and consumers. Directive 2006/112/EC, VAT Directive, which was adopted by the Council of the European Union in December 2017 and November 2019 are the core of this package. The main problem to address the directives being implemented is the unsatisfactory efficiency of VAT collection. This is due in particular to the fact that, to a large extent, such sales are carried out by a very large group of non-established entrepreneurs or permanent establishments in the European Union. The Law and Justice Club will vote in favour of this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u20	Thank you very much, sir. Mr Dariusz Wieczorek, parliamentary club of the Left, has the floor. Mr Darek Supper doesn't have a voice because he's not here. ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u21	Ladies and gentlemen! This draft law adapts Polish law to European Union legislation. The aim of the new solutions is to seal VAT and overcome barriers to cross-border e-commerce between traders and consumers. At present, a significant proportion of the sales of goods to consumers in the Union, both within the framework of trade between Member States and in respect of goods imported into the Union from third countries, are made via an electronic trading platform, portal or other similar measures. This is often done using warehouses located in the territory of the Union providing comprehensive logistics services. To a large extent, such sales are carried out by a very large group of businesses with no establishment or permanent establishment in the Union. The proposed amendments are intended to address the problem of low VAT collection efficiency and, at the same time, the relatively high administrative burden and costs on traders and the tax administration in relation to the settlement of the tax due on transactions and the verification of the correct accounting. Changes in practice are intended to benefit national sellers and reduce the tax fraud that has so far arisen. The amendments or solutions proposed in the Act concern, first, the uniform registration threshold throughout the Union. This package assumes that for mail order sales a uniform turnover threshold will be set for all Member States, up to which VAT can be cleared in the supplier's country and at the applicable rate. Secondly, they relate to VAT accounting in one declaration. Third, the elimination of import exemptions in the Union is intended to restore the competitive balance between suppliers in the Union and third country suppliers. Finally, fourthly, changes are being made in the case of online platforms. These are new obligations for online purchasing platforms between consumers from the Union and non-Union suppliers or suppliers who will offer imported goods from outside the Union. On the basis of new solutions that will start to apply on 1 July, the online platforms will take on a major role in sealing VAT rules within the Union in the transactions that are being carried out. Thus, these solutions are not only adapted to the requirements and to the solutions introduced under EU directives and procedures, but also have a significant impact on the volume of VAT resources. Thank you so much for your attention. [[Applause]]

We know very well - on these pages, in connection with electronics auctions - that the packages are so marked, so transported: by France, by Hamburg, that it can be avoided that the customs officer will keep this package and add VAT and customs duties. And Poles use this option massly. It is unfavorable for Polish entrepreneurs who are engaged in the import of various things, among others. electronics, selling it in Poland. Since it is more profitable to buy such a telephone from China, directly, without VAT, without duty, why pay for the margin of the Polish distributor? And that's what makes you want to seal the system. But be aware of how this will affect the consumer: Poles now, after sealing this system, will not be able to use this gate, which they used so massively, and thus for many Poles who have so far been shopping on Chinese portals, such purchases will be more expensive, the prices of these products will be increased accordingly: VAT, very often also customs. So this stick has two ends. Therefore, it should be considered whether taxes are a good solution or whether we need to have this high VAT. If high VAT makes it worthwhile to conspire to deliver online purchases in such a way as to avoid this VAT, perhaps it would be good to consider whether or not this VAT would simply be reduced. It cannot be completely eliminated because you have agreed to European VAT rules, but it can be reduced to 15%. Perhaps this would be better than playing cat and mouse, constantly racing about patching up a system that entrepreneurs - if that's what you can call this zone, a gray zone - avoid so that citizens, i.e. we all, our neighbors, our friends, can buy things cheaply abroad. ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u27	Thank you kindly. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the pleasure to present on behalf of the Parliamentary Circle Poland 2050 a position on the government draft law amending the Act on VAT on goods and services and on certain other laws included in print No 1114. Ladies and gentlemen! This amendment is so-called. e-commerce VAT package. It aims to implement the provisions of Directive 2017/2455 of the Council of the European Union and Directive 2019/1995 of the Council of the European Union. The aim of these solutions is to seal the VAT system in cross-border e-commerce between traders and consumers. In particular, it is about regulating the sale of goods to consumers in the European Union in the framework of trade between Member States, as well as for goods imported into the Union from third countries purchased via the Internet. The rules apply to all Community and non-Community traders who sell goods and services electronically to natural persons in the European Union irrespective of the electronic sales channel. The most important changes to these provisions include: Introducing a defined concept of intra-Community distance selling of goods, extending and modifying the special scheme for telecommunications services, broadcasting services or electronically supplied services, defining the concept of distance selling of goods imported from third countries, extending simplification in the form of so-called. a small one-stop shop for distance sales of goods imported from third countries in consignments of an actual value not exceeding EUR 150 for EU consumers, so-called. import they stop shop, introduce in cases where this does not apply a simplified procedure for declaring and deferring tax payments on imports of goods in consignments of a value not exceeding EUR 150, introduce a tax exemption on goods and services for imports of goods in consignments of a value not exceeding EUR 150 if this tax is declared and settled in the procedure I have just mentioned. The biggest changes will be affected by electronic platforms which, by intermediary sales to European Union consumers, can be considered as a participant in the transaction. As a result, they will be obliged to collect and settle VAT in the cases indicated. It is important for EU entrepreneurs to harmonise the 10 000 threshold across the European Union. the euro, after which the place of VAT will change. Substantial changes also apply to sellers outside the European Union whose goods will be more expensive for VAT, basically from the first zloty. The clear consequence of the proposed measures will be an increase in tax revenue. According to EU officials, this could be around EUR 7 billion throughout the Community. The Minister was just talking about the possibility that this could be about PLN 1 billion for our country, for additional revenue to the budget. Of course, this is good information on the one hand, but it is also worth remembering that it will affect prices, i.e. government proposals will de facto lead to price increases in parts of transactions made electronically by consumers. However, attention should be paid to the fact that, from the point of view of maintaining a level playing field in the market, it is extremely important for a participant to move within the same rules. The development of e-commerce has created disparities between different actors. Those companies that operate outside the European Union do not fall within the scope of regulation or even circumvent regulations, benefit from gaps, do not apply the same rules. This made them in a much better position than EU companies. Of course, this creates a distortion of competitiveness. Community companies are in a worse position and this is damaging the EU economy as a whole. These rules therefore seem to be correct, but the government has not escaped a certain error in proposing excessive obligations to document transactions. It introduces a condition for the VIS to be regarded as a supply carried out in the territory of a Member State, to terminate the dispatch or transport of goods to the purchaser where the goods are in the territory of the country at the time of their dispatch or transport. The condition is to present a series of evidence. [[♪ The bell ♪]] However, we feel that this obligation is too far-reaching, as the directive itself imposes an obligation to register transactions. According to Poland 2050 there is no need to further complicate the lives of our entrepreneurs, but on the contrary - they need to be facilitated. Therefore, on behalf of the Parliamentary Circle, Poland 2050 I request that the project be submitted to the committee in order to find better solutions. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u51	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I will start with the first questions that have emerged: Who will pay this over 1 billion a year? To answer the question about surveillance, access to information, transaction statements, I would like to highlight two things. Firstly, the regulations we are introducing are entirely based on EU rules and will work in Poland exactly the same way as in other countries in the Community. The proposal itself appeared at the request of Germany, while Poland did not oppose the implementation of this information umbrella because it was done at EU level in a very safe way. Information on transactions will be stored by entrepreneurs and made available, on request, to tax administrations from Poland or other countries of the European Union, which will examine, of course, where necessary, whether the trader has actually carried out intra-EU transactions. What is very important is that there will be no information about individual consumers; there will only be information about sellers and the volume of their transactions. Are we implementing these rules late? We implement them on time. We have joined this work under very difficult COVID conditions. The technical assumptions for the project were only made available by the European Commission at the end of 2019 in the last months of this year. The project was ready and consulted in October last year, i.e. practically at the summit of the second wave of COVID and despite the third wave, the rules went to the Council of Ministers in April and were adopted by it and could be held at the session of the Sejm at the previous meeting. However, even considering them now does not mean that this project is at risk and that it will be implemented after time. With regard to the question of reducing VAT from 23% to 22%, I will explain that the rules for this step are laid down in statutory tools and, together with the improved economic situation, the rate will automatically fall from 23% to 22%. On the other hand, I would like to emphasise that the COVID Summit itself, in July 2020, saw a reform of the VAT rates, the implementation of a new matrix, which significantly reduced the tax on many first-time goods: bread, fruit, even baby diapers. So that VAT is already noticeably lower on these most important issues. As regards Mrs Skowronska's question, I would like to clarify that VAT in Germany is not 16% , and 19%. VAT rates in Poland and Germany are the result of these countries' tax policies and, as VAT in Germany is lower by 3 percentage points, e.g. CIT is 15% , and in Poland for as much as 93% taxpayers - only 9%. For those producing goods in Poland and registered it is even lower, it is 5% So it's not a third, but three times lower than in Germany. As far as customs matters are concerned, they remain at the total margin here, as also the revenue from customs duties goes to the vast majority of the EU budget and not to the Polish budget. Hence, this is not the subject of this debate. As far as the impact of these solutions on the Polish tax system and on the state of public finances is concerned, as I mentioned to you, only this year an additional 0.5 billion funds in the budget were downloaded almost 100% from large portals, far-eastern corporations, which so far earned on unpaid tax in Poland - annually around 1.2 billion. Now Polish entrepreneurs will benefit most, those who had to compete with large producers from the Far East - who did not pay this tax - and who could not compete with entities selling exactly the same goods, but cheaper by a quarter. This problem will be significantly reduced as of 1 July. So that this is very good information for Polish entrepreneurs and also fulfilling their demands, because many voices from the market were sent to the Ministry of Finance so that we would not be late with this implementation, but so far there is no such threat. ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u53	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Council of Ministers shall present to the High House a draft law which is part of the regulation which forms a market part of the housing package. It is a draft law on amending the Act on certain forms of housing promotion and some other laws which introduces social rental agencies, called SAN, into Polish legal trade. The social leasing agencies are implementing a model of cooperation consisting of mediation between housing owners and tenants who meet the economic and social criteria set by the municipality. This solution will be an additional instrument for municipalities, which aims to increase the set of housing policy tools, and for medium- and low-income people it will be an additional way to meet housing needs. An additional argument for the implementation of SAN into the Polish legal order is the current situation related to the pandemic, which also damaged the functioning of the housing market for rent. According to various estimates, there are 600 000 in this market. up to 1.2 million apartments in Poland. Due to the absence on the market of rentals of students or employees and due to restrictions that affected foreign workers working in Poland, some of the apartment owners lost their tenants. SAN provides in this situation the possibility to use existing housing within the framework of municipal housing policy, ensuring that income sources are maintained. The draft law includes the definition of a social rental agency and regulations which define the rules for concluding contracts between individual participants implementing the SAN model, i.e. the commune, the social rental agency, the owner of the apartment and the tenant. In addition, the Act sets out the scope of the resolution in which the municipality determines inter alia. criteria for persons who will be able to apply for an apartment in SAN. Entities carrying out tasks as SAN, such as associations, foundations or social cooperatives, will cooperate with the municipality on the basis of the provisions of the Act on Public Use and Volunteering. The social leasing agencies will also be municipal companies, which will make use of the potential of existing structures, e.g. TBS, SIM or day-to-day management of municipal housing. In order to encourage housing owners to lease their premises to rental agencies, the project proposes tax exemptions for the rental income for SAN. As a result of the guarantee of regular payment of rent and the reduction of social charges, rental agencies are expected to be able to negotiate rent rates below 20% of rent rates. In order to further reduce the rents of SAN tenants, the proposed law introduces the possibility of using the rent subsidies that are available in the program The amount of these payments depends on the level of market prices at the location and on the number of persons in the household. As part of the model of operation of SAN, it is envisaged that the municipalities will have the task of adopting a resolution setting out the criteria for applying for an agreement with SAN and signing a cooperation agreement with SAN. SAN's task will be to lease the apartment on the market and conclude a contract with tenants designated by the municipality. It will also be possible for SAN to coordinate the renovation of the premises in exchange for a temporary reduction in the rental or temporary cessation of its collection. Given the experience of running SAN through the Habitat for Humanity Poland Foundation and the City Office of Najmu in Poznań, one can see the significant potential of the SAN model. It is fully visible when with the rental service customers SAN can also count on social services, such as helping to apply for benefits or support in the search for work. In addition, the Poznań experience indicates that leasing mediation was of great interest not only from potential tenants but also from apartment owners, while preliminary concerns about irregular payment of rent by tenants were unjustified. This project, which is the result of work involving experienced actors involved in the housing sector and local government units, has been particularly interested by local authorities, who face various challenges in housing and see the possibility of solving at least part of the problems of housing policy in the SAN formula. Of course, this instrument cannot be regarded as a basic tool for implementing local housing policies, but it is a complement to other instruments already proposed in the first housing package, such as: funding for municipal or social construction. In view of the benefits presented from the implementation of the SAN model into a set of instruments allowing municipal policies to be implemented, [[♪ The bell ♪]] I bring on behalf of the Council of Ministers a positive opinion on the draft law amending the Act on certain forms of housing promotion and certain other laws by the High House. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u59	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the parliamentary club of the Left, I have the honour to present to the High House our position on the draft law amending the Act on certain forms of housing promotion and certain other laws contained in print No 1070. Unfortunately, the pandemic period is a period of some inhibition in the housing market. The work of remote, remote studios, the lack of more tourist traffic caused that much less people rent apartments today. Many apartments are empty, not to mention that almost 2 million apartments are missing in Poland. The aim of the projector is therefore to use the oversupply of housing in the market for social rent. The Act introduces a legal model of mediation between housing owners and natural persons who want to rent apartments and who cannot afford to rent apartments on the commercial market. The solution to this problem is, according to the government, the Social Agency for the Nation, in short, SAN. The agency is supposed to be a panacea to solve the problem of oversupply of apartments for rent. The aim of the Social Agency Najmu is to acquire apartments on the market mainly from individuals. SAN offers apartment owners a long lease with a guarantee of rent, which will reduce the rate of rent by up to 20% as a result of negotiations, as shown by Western examples, as well as by reducing VAT. These apartments will then be rented to those in need. SAN is intended to be a tenant of residential premises or residential buildings from their owners and then a tenant of an apartment designated by the municipality to tenants, provided that they meet the criteria set out in the decision of the municipality. SAN will be able to set up a company with majority participation of the municipality, non-governmental organisation, foundation, association or social cooperative as a public benefit entity. The entire income of SAN pursuant to the bill is to be allocated to statutory activities. The Act sets out the rules for the termination and conclusion of contracts between SAN and the owners of leased premises. As an incentive to enter the owners of the dwellings into this system, the legislator, as I said earlier, provided for them to exempt these operations from VAT. The tenants of the housing units under the responsibility of the Social Housing Agency will be able to benefit from the rent subsidies under the government's scheme of living at the start. The proposed solutions may be an alternative to municipal construction, but not entirely. My parliamentary club welcomes the bill presented, especially when, Minister, the government draws the model from the left-wing actions that take place in Poznań, because there has already been a similar institution. That's where a similar system has been operating for 3 years at the City Bureau of Najmu. The office offers the maximum rent to owners of 1200 PLN gross regardless of the area of the apartment. The tenant pays 40%. the value of the rent, while after the amendment of the Act on the Protection of the Rights of Tenants, the rent was further reduced. However, the lease was stopped due to the fact that first contracts were institutional and concluded for a fixed period and, after the change of law, became contracts for an indefinite period, which led to a loss of interest on the part of the owners. After the entry into force of the SAN Act, the lease may again begin, as indicated by the great interest of the owners of the housing estates. Today, apartment owners avoid renting apartments to mothers with small children with disabilities due to the Act on the Protection of the Rights of Tenants. They're afraid that in the event of payment problems they won't be able to get people out of the premises. If the intermediary is the Social Agency for the Nest, this risk will not be present, especially when the participation of the municipality is assured, as the agency will take the burden on itself. As the financial data of the City Nest Office in Poznań show, the rent arrears under this system are very low. We hope that the creation of the Social Agency for Najmu will also in some way help foreigners who come to us from abroad permanently and become members of our community, so they should have the right to benefit from urban programs and therefore also from the offer of SAN. I would like to say that the solution to the housing problem of people who cannot afford to pay commercial rent is very much at the heart of the Left. E.g. That is why we have sought to ensure that the National Reconstruction Plan contains provisions for 75 000. apartments for rent. Today's law will allow for a reduction in rent, but it also saves in some way the people who have purchased apartments that have become part of their property today. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the parliamentary club of the Left, I request that this project (the bell) be forwarded to the Infrastructure Committee for further work.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u118	Thank you very much, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u119	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice in relation to the Act amending the Act - Law on the Public Prosecutor's Office, the Law on Public Finance and some other laws, print no. 1086. As the Minister stated, the management of the deposit sums obtained in connection with proceedings conducted or supervised in the general organisational units of the prosecutor's office is not currently regulated. These amounts include the amounts of asset guarantees, the funds included in the asset security or retained as evidence in kind in connection with criminal proceedings being conducted or supervised. In addition, the deposit sums covered by the proposed regulation also include the deposit sums related to the day-to-day operation of the office units of the prosecutor's office, such as liabilities or collateral for the proper performance of the contract. This directory is open. Keeping deposit sums in different ways - because there is no regulation here so far - makes it difficult to control and obtain data about them, and makes it different for individual entities to be treated according to which entity conducts or supervises the proceedings concerned. The proposed law proposes that the deposit sums obtained in connection with proceedings conducted or supervised in general units, inter alia. the prosecutor's office or with the day-to-day operation of those units were held in the deposit accounts of the Minister of Finance, Funds and Regional Policy held by Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego, on the same terms What are the court's deposits? In order to achieve this, you need a design regulation to introduce and amend several laws, among others. in the Law - Law on Lawyer, in the Law on Public Finance, in the Enforcement Penal Code, in the Law - Law on the System of Universal Courts. The proposed provision provides that the accounts will be kept in gold, in euros, US dollars, UK pounds, Swiss francs. Until now, according to the Act, these accounts have been kept in any foreign currency, but in practice Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego has kept these accounts in these five proposed currencies. The funds in other currencies listed in the national bank's exchange rate table or cash intermediary of the national bank will be paid into deposit accounts held in zlotys at the currency's rate. The deposit sums of the public prosecutor's office will have to be transferred to the deposit accounts of the Minister of Finance, Funds and Regional Policy maintained by Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego within 6 months of the entry into force of the proposed law. Deposit sums held in bank accounts, interest-bearing such as fixed-term deposits, will have to be transferred to deposit accounts of the Minister of Finance, Funds and Regional Policy maintained by Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego no later than 14 days after the end of the deposit. As I have put it, the proposed solution is necessary. The Law and Justice Club will therefore support this solution in the further work of the House. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u143	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Health Committee, I would like to present a report on the government draft law amending the Law on the provision of public-funded healthcare and certain other laws. This report is contained in print No 1122. The law, which was submitted by the government, was addressed to the Members in print no. 1013. At its meeting on 15 April, in accordance with the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, the Sejm referred the above draft to the Health Committee for review. The Health Commission has met twice in this matter. At the first meeting on 13 April, the first reading was held. The committee adopted a proposal to reject the project at first reading. The Sejm did not share the views of the Members, the committee and sent the draft to be re-examined. We met on April 22nd and re-examined the entire bill. What's the bill about? This bill is basically an ordering act. I don't really understand what the opposition is talking about when it comes to some sort of centralization. Well, health plans, health maps exist in Poland and were introduced exactly in August 2014. - so not by this government, not by this parliamentary team - in the benefit law. It assumed that at the level of voivodships specific maps of health needs were created. This law is ordering and these health maps will, of course, continue to be constructed at this level, but the entire preparation procedure and supervision will be directed centrally by the Minister of Health. Poland is not a Federation country, it is a single country. In Poland, in Podkarpacie, in Silesia or in West Pomerania, COVID suffers from a similar illness, more or less similar is epidemiology, so there is no reason not to create a single map of health needs, of course under the aegis of those who have the highest competence and substantive skills. The Minister of Health has basically commissioned three very important institutions: the National Institute of Public Health, the former Polish Hygiene Institute, which enjoys more than 100 years of tradition, an institute that deals with public health, the National Health Fund, because for 18 years he has been carrying all the data on diseases, epidemiology, costs, etc., and the Agency for Medical Technology Assessment and Tarification, because for several years she has been engaged not only in assessing the suitability of the treatment data, but also in their taxiing, i.e. determining the costs. Of course, the plans will be mainly directed by voivodship centres and voivodships, while the Minister of Health will be decisive, based on the criteria contained in this amendment of the law. One new thing that's here, and I think it's very right, is what's called a transformation plan, a national transformation plan. This is new. Why do we need maps if we can't do them? The maps existed, and no one could realize them specifically, because they were from Sas to Las and not necessarily compatible in the environment of our entire country. These transformation plans, the national transformation, they assume one thing. Well, ladies and gentlemen, we've been in a pandemic situation for over a year now, and we already know that classic treatments, classic medical procedures, will be rapidly accelerated. Who else a few years ago thought telemedicine would come in with such great momentum? And this telemedicine we're not gonna get rid of, she's gonna be with us, because it improves a lot of diagnostics and treatment processes. Therefore, these voivodship health needs plans will be reviewed, evaluated by the institutions I mentioned, and the Minister will decide what health needs plans are, at the same time to be able to create those transformation changes that are necessary, i.e. the appropriate facilities and infrastructure, people, the need for medical technology, also not everywhere, but in selected centres, because we know that some medical therapies can be very expensive and therefore must be dedicated where they are best performed. During the committee's work, we accepted some amendments from Members of the opposition. I would like to thank the Left, in particular, who has bothered and tabled certain amendments. ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u144	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Ms Anna Dąbrowska-Banaszek will speak on behalf of the Parliamentary Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u145	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice regarding the government draft law amending the Law on the provision of health care financed by public funds and certain other laws, print No. 1013, as amended, print No. 1122. The bill under investigation is an amendment to the Law of 27 August 2004 on public-funded healthcare provision. In particular, it aims to improve the system of strategic planning for health care, i.e. to introduce organisational changes to improve the operation of the system and to ensure equal access to public-funded healthcare services. It changes the procedure for mapping health needs and introduces transformation plans, national and voivodship, in place of priorities for regional health policy. The Minister and local government units will develop a draft health policy programme on the basis of a map of health needs and available epidemiological data. The Health Need Map will be a key document to identify health needs and challenges for the organisation of the healthcare system. It will be the foundation of the creation of a national transformation plan. The national transition plan will be developed, established, monitored and updated by the Minister of Health. It will take into account recommended directions of action indicated on the map and resulting from other strategy papers in health care. It will cover activities within 5 years with a mid-term evaluation in the third year of validity. It will identify health needs, challenges, planned year or years in which they will be implemented, implementation actors, estimated costs, expected results, indicators of implementation of individual actions. The Minister will forward the draft national transformation plan to the Social Dialogue Council, the President of the Fund, the President of the Agency and the Director of the National Institute of Public Health - State Hygiene Institute, National Health Consultants, the Director of the National Institute of Cardiology, the Director of the National Institute of Oncology for Opinion, with a deadline of 30 days from the date of receipt of the opinion. Voivodship transformation plans will be drawn up by voivodships for voivodship areas, taking into account the national transformation plan and recommended directions of actions indicated on the map and resulting from other strategic documents in health care, in cooperation with the voivodship council established to develop this project for health needs. This plan will identify health needs, challenges for coordinated actions at voivodship level, actions themselves, planned year or years in which these activities will be implemented, entities responsible for implementation, estimated cost, expected results. Wojewóda will present the project of the provincial plan to the voivodship marshal, the convention of powiats of the given voivodship, the voivodship consultants in health care, the president of the fund and the regional council of social dialogue for opinion, with a deadline for submitting comments: 30 days from the date of receipt. It will then forward this proposal to assess the Minister responsible for Health by the last day of February of the year preceding the first year of application of the plan. The Minister responsible for Health shall, within 3 months of receipt of the voivodship plan project, evaluate the project in terms of compliance with the map, purpose, adequacy of the actions adopted and then approve the voivodship plan project or transmit comments to that project to the voivodship. Health care in Poland does not work well. The contested law will allow directional changes to be made. This law does not refer to specific methods of diagnosis, treatment, but sets out how the necessary changes are to be made in order to ensure equal access to public-funded healthcare for all. The state must provide patients with a universal and efficient comprehensive health care system that facilitates daily life. It is important to cooperate with medical providers at all levels, i.e. family doctor, rescue system, county hospitals, voivodship and clinical hospitals. The transformation plans that have been developed can give this an orderly character. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u206	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. I invite Mr Andrzej Gawron from the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice. There you go.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u207	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the Government's draft law amending the Renewable Energy Sources Act and some other laws, print No 1129, and report, print No 1173. At the end of 2020 there were more than 450,000 connected to the network in Poland. photovoltaic micro-installations. Maintaining the current pace of development of renewable energy projects, especially photovoltaic and wind projects, requires a clear, stable state policy framework for the development of renewable energy sources. The objective is to achieve a gross RES share of 21% to 23% by 2030 in the final energy consumption. Most of this energy consumption is set out in the National Energy and Climate Plan. This document also forecasts the necessary investment in electricity generation capacity in 2021-2030 at more than EUR 17 billion, of which nearly 75% will be the expenditure on wind and photovoltaic sources. This amendment is moving towards the necessary modification of the regulatory framework for further dynamic development of renewable energy sources. The main objective of the amendment is to reduce the concession obligations for small-scale entrepreneurs. This will be done by increasing the upper threshold of the total installed electricity or heat output combined for renewable energy installations. According to the amendment, a small installation will be an installation of a renewable energy source whose capacity is higher than 50 kW but not more than 1 MW. Some existing manufacturers exempt from the obligation to hold concessions would be automatically entered in the register of small installations' business activities. The amendment will, on the one hand, reduce the obligations for entities wishing to generate energy in small installations, on the other hand, result in the transfer of undertakings holding a concession from the office for which installations meet the conditions set for small installations to the register of energy producers in a small installation. The project therefore includes key elements related to the auction system in the legislation, namely the power of installed electricity. As regards land-use change, the project is very positive in changing the existing rules according to which PV farms could be built in the spatial planning plan to 100 KW, and now, thanks to the revision of this project, this limit will be shifted to 500 KW and in some cases even 1000 KW, if these are free-standing installations on specific land, which are also indicated by the law. At present, it is estimated that about 15% installations on the market are single phase installations. The legislator decided to clarify the provisions more clearly in order to treat three-phase and single-phase installations in a similar way. The aim of the proposed amendments to the Act is to revive the economy and respond to the social expectations of local government units and entrepreneurs who want to invest in this area. They are also an important solution, in particular for energy producers from the entire RES installation industry. At the same time, these solutions are becoming an effective part of the process of exiting the economic crisis related to the COVID-19 pandemic, in which the development of renewable energy is given a significant role. On the other hand, economic development and activation of local communities can contribute to economic growth and increase revenue to the budget of both local and state units. Based on the above facts and taking into account the concern for the dynamic development of renewable energy in Poland, which will improve the required energy balance and positively affect our environment, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the proposed amendment and will vote in favour of it. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u208	Thank you, sir. Mr Paweł Papke, Parliamentary Club Civic Coalition. There you go.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u209	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the opinion of the parliamentary club of the Civic Coalition on the government draft law amending the Renewable Energy Sources Act and some other laws, prints no. 1129 and 1173. This is an amendment to the Act of 20 February 2015 on Renewable Energy Sources, which aims to reduce the licensing obligations of economic operators operating in the field of so-called "business." a small installation. The problem was that there were no provisions in the Act on Practices relating to how to determine the total capacity of the installed RES electrical installation. Given the change in the qualification of the concession obligation to registered RES power generators for several thousand installations, this issue becomes extremely important and necessary for regulatory regulation. The Act therefore sets out the parameters of the auction system of the electrical capacity of the installation in question, while remaining in line with the practice used so far. The aim of the changes is, among others, to make changes.: limiting the obligations of entities wishing to generate energy in small installations, transferring undertakings with concessions and having installations known as small to the register of energy producers in small installations. The proposed amendments will introduce a simplified formula for increasing the number of entities with an entry in the register of energy generators in a small installation. Operating at the end of 2020 more than 450,000 the micro-installation requires changes to the law that will enable them to develop. Observed the development of this topic within the framework of the so-called. the discharge system clearly indicates that the changes are required to ensure a uniform treatment of the settlement of renewable energy prosumers. The amendments to the Act also refer to the promotion of renewable energy sources in the formula of energy cooperatives, which are in vain sought in Poland and whose rules of operation in this formula will be comparable to prosumer production of renewable energy. It is necessary to establish a state policy framework for the development of renewable energy sources, as by 2030 the share of renewable energy in gross energy consumption is expected to reach between 21 and 23%. , which was included in the National Energy and Climate Plan 2021-2030. The Act regulates issues relating to the definition of a long-term timetable for the provision of support to RES generators as a reference point for 6 consecutive years. It refers to the quantity and value of electricity from renewable energy sources sold by auction in the years 2022 to 2027. This value cannot be reduced. This gives an opportunity to define the level of development of the RES sector and to define the investment perspective. Other amendments to the Act respond to factors related to the prevention, prevention and eradication of COVID-19, including:: Clarification that the undertaking participating in the auction is not in economic difficulty or the definition of safeguards when the deadline for the first generation of electricity or first sale is exceeded. Photovoltaic installations may replace old central heating and water systems. The use of this type of equipment is an alternative to the development of industrial, post-police and poor quality agricultural land, as well as roofs of all construction sites. The Civic Coalition has always supported all actions leading to green energy production, and what this entails - environmental care and natural resources. After yesterday's multi-hour discussion in the Committee on Energy, Climate and State Assets, during which there was a slight chaos on the side of the government and at the last moment amendments to the project were proposed, I state that the Civic Coalition Parliamentary Club supports this project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u266	Thank you very much, sir. I would now like to ask Mrs Anita Sowinska, who will present the position of the Left Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u267	Dear Marshal, Dear Minister, High Izbo! One more question... No, no, not you. May I? Dear Marshal, Dear Minister, High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Coalition Parliamentary Club Left on the government draft law amending the law on ownership transformations in the sugar industry. As indicated by the project applicant, the purpose of this regulation is to consolidate agri-food companies. It is intended to run on the basis of the National Sugar Company, which is the largest company with the participation of the State Treasury in this industry. This bill is a government project, which means that it was consulted in the process of the Government Legislative Centre. The project did not receive any major social comments, and in fact only one comment was made by the trade union. The Left respects the opinion of the trade unions and sugar beet growers, which is why we also want to support this project. But the devil is in the details. I would like to draw your attention to the broader, important context of this project. At yesterday's committee meeting, Minister Ślivka confirmed that this law is a prerequisite for the establishment of the National Food Holding. It will be a company that will run outside the sugar industry, i.e. its activities may also apply to, for example. the market for fruit and vegetables, meat or other agricultural products. The aim of the food holding company is to influence the competitiveness of Polish companies in the international market through optimization of financial management. Let us remember, however, that there are risks to this that you will not learn from official government communications. The first risk is for farmers and is linked to the imbalance of power on the market. The strong position of one company means that fragmented farmers will have less to say. A large player may dictate buying-in prices for beet or other agricultural products. As a result, farmers may - not necessarily, but can - earn less. We see this in the example of the vegetable and fruit market, where the farmer earns just a dozen percent of the final price of the product, and most of the profit is taken by intermediaries and large-area shops. If a large entity is even bigger, it can dictate lower prices for the purchase of agricultural fruits, and thus saves more money for itself. And this is how this optimization of financial management, which I mentioned earlier, works. The second risk is for shareholders and is that the role of a large state shareholder is increasing in relation to the shares of farmers and employees of the company. Currently, the State Treasury has 76% shares. Imagine this: Orlen's president is having a dream and says that from tomorrow he will invest in carrots, sugar or chicken. After the takeover of the Polish Press, nothing will surprise me. Suppose Orlen buys the shares of a sugar company. It can do this, it has a lot of money as opposed to farmers and unlike workers, i.e. employees' participation will decrease and they will have less impact on the company's activities. The third risk concerns all of us and involves how a holding company can spend its earned money. In a normal country, I would have no doubt: a sugar company should be involved in sugar processing and selling it, and a fuel and energy company should be involved in oil processing and investing in RES. But not in Poland, we do not live in a normal country. ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u291	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Sejm Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, I would like to present a report to the committee on the examination of the government draft law amending the Act on the Production and Trading of Explosives, Arms, Ammunition, Military or Police Purpose Products and Technology, 1085 Seym Printing. The Marshal sent this draft to the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs for first reading on 14 April 2021. The law on its regulation implements the directive... [[Game in the O.R.]] Excuse me, sir. Can we ask Members to respect the rapporteur? Yes. Thank you. The law on its regulation implements European Commission Implementing Directive 68/2019 of 16 January 2019 laying down technical specifications for the marking of firearms and their essential components in accordance with Council Directive 91/477/EEC on the control, acquisition and possession of weapons and the European Commission Implementing Directive of 16 January 2019 laying down technical specifications for alarm and signal weapons in accordance with Directive 91/477/EEC on the control of the acquisition and possession of weapons. The most important solutions contained in the Act are that firearms and their essential parts are to be marked with a clear, durable and unique marking in accordance with the technical specifications for the marking of firearms and their essential parts. Those specifications are set out in the Annex to the Directive. The type of alphabet and the minimum size of the font and marking of small arms and their essential parts have been established. The law sets out the methods permitted by the State for the marking of small arms and their essential parts, with particular regard to the way in which the marking is applied on the skeleton or lock chamber of small firearms made of non-metallic materials. According to the Act, the device will be considered to be alarm and signal weapons and will not be considered to be firearms if the State-approved entity verifies and issues a certificate that it is an alarm and signal weapon manufactured in accordance with Union technical specifications. The authorized entity will be designated in the Regulation to the Act, and it is assumed to be the Central Police Criminal Laboratory. The Act sets out rules for the marking of alarm and signal weapons, including the marking of certain parts thereof, the type of alphabet and the size of the font of the marking. This also applies to an additional sign indicating that it is an alarm and signal weapon and its creation and placing on the market in Poland has been subject to national control. At the same time, the Act will establish a National Contact Point for Emergency and Signal Weapons, the main task of which will be to exchange information on the types and models of alarm and signal weapons manufactured and controlled by Member States. The new regulations are to enter into force 14 days after the announcement in the Official Journal of the Laws. The Committee on Administration and Home Affairs carried out its first reading, examining this draft at its meeting on 12 May. During the committee's work, it was pointed out that the project should also be examined by the Committee on National Defence and perhaps by the economic committee on the basis of the matter concerned. In addition to the drafting and substantive amendments tabled by the Legislative Bureau of the Sejm, four substantive amendments were tabled, two of which were found by the members of the committee and clarified the definition of a specific part of the weapon. During the committee's work, perhaps an inaccurate translation into the Polish language of the directive was noted, especially since these matters are specialised. It was also found that, during the second reading, the government would propose amendments that would clarify these issues. As a result of the vote, the Commission adopted the report in print No 1149 submitted to the High House and proposes that the draft submitted be adopted. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u292	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u293	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I have the honour to present a position on the Government amendment of the Act on the Production and Trading of Explosives, Arms, Ammunition and Military or Police Purpose Products and Technology. The changes made, as stressed by the host of the law, namely the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration, are due to the need to clarify the rules which need to be interpreted. They are also due to the intention to fully implement EU implementing directives in the Polish legal order - one which concerns technical specifications related to the marking of firearms and their essential components, and another which concerns technical specifications for alarm and signal weapons. Both implementing Directives should be implemented, fully implemented by the Member States of the European Union by 17 January 2020. The amendment of the bill introduces control of alarm and signal weapons. At the same time, however, it stresses that the trading of such weapons does not require a concession. The amendment amended the definition of ammunition, including its components, which do not contain explosives - bodies of artillery ammunition shells and shell casings, mine hulls, propulsion systems components, guidance and control of missiles, aerial and deep-sea bombs, torpedoes and target imitators. A very important element of the amendment is the need for each substantial piece of firearms to be labelled by the manufacturer as soon as it is manufactured, whether it is to be part of the firearms or to be placed on the market separately. Personally, I remember very well that when working on the law on the manufacture and trafficking of weapons and ammunition, the requirement that it has its origin in the EU directive was the source of many controversy and doubts expressed by entrepreneurs. It has been pointed out that the English version of the Directive contains a provision allowing for the interpretation of this requirement in such a way as to make it necessary to mark arms or any substantial part thereof as soon as they were manufactured. Unfortunately, the position of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs on the obligation to mark arms and their essential parts and the position of the European Commission on this issue has resulted from the fact that the obligation to mark all essential components has its origin for the general purpose of the directive and its systematics, and that it is therefore necessary to implement the necessity of marking all essential parts of weapons, whether they are part of firearms or will be placed on the market separately. (062...062) This amendment also includes a change in this respect. Other changes include: defining the way in which firearms and alarm and signal weapons are labelled - these are the issues that clarify and clarify - or the requirement that no other activities are carried out in places dedicated to weapons storage. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to inform you that the Law and Justice Club will support this draft amendment of the law in the vote. At the same time, on behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I make four amendments.

Contrary to appearances, it is difficult to proceed with this law, hence the voices emerged in order to appoint a subcommittee and invite representatives of the three ministries to work in it, in accordance with their competences. Experts and committee members have repeatedly stressed the poor quality of the translation of the text of the directive, which raises a number of doubts, including interpretations, which, after all, are to be eliminated by the law. The bill is crawling with imprecise, questionable wordings, especially when discussing the definition of specific types of small arms. Experts have shown that Polish professional nicknames do not contain such terms and names as those used by the authors of the law. Many emotions have aroused the definition of alarm-signal weapons, which is defined differently in different places of the legal act and otherwise in the law on weapons and ammunition. Once you talk about firearms, once you talk about firearms, another time you talk about alarm weapons, while experts say it's enough to use the word bang weapons or acoustic weapons, because that's what the directive says. This seemingly trivial matter concerns many people, even those who practice sailing, because on every yacht there must be an alarm-signal weapon, or a popular rocket launcher. On the occasion of the vote on the amendment to include scales in the category of ammunition, Article. 1 point 2 of the contested law, there was a long discussion. There was a fear that the use of provisions proposed by the government side would result in militari pickers exploring battlefields being charged with illegal possession of ammunition, and at the antiquities fair there would be manhunts on craftsmen selling vases made of bullet casings. The Act imposes new obligations on the Chief Police Officer for the creation and conduct of the so-called police. contact points of the Signal and Alarm Weapons Register, new obligations for entrepreneurs to obtain paid certificates of the nature of the weapons in question. The Central Police Crime Laboratory is basically the only unit in the country that has the facilities to issue such certificates. The government's representative reassured that all the expertise provided by this law would be paid to economic operators, so there is no requirement the transfer of additional funds to him. However, there is concern that the new responsibilities will not affect the quality of the laboratory's work in its key tasks. Despite these reservations, the Civic Coalition - Civic Platform will support this law, but amends it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u297	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The purpose of the amendment is to implement two European instruments into the Polish legal order: Commission Implementing Directive (EU) 2019/68 of 16 January laying down technical specifications for the marking of firearms and their essential components, in accordance with the Council Directive on the control of the acquisition and possession of weapons, and the Commission Implementing Directive laying down technical specifications for alarm and signal weapons, in accordance with the Council Directive on the control of the acquisition and possession of weapons. As regards the implementing acts laying down technical specifications for the marking of firearms and the specifications for alarm and signal weapons, work has been carried out on the European Commission's replacement of the Firearms Committee. The main provisions of the Directive address the following issues: marking and traceability of weapons history, requirements for the acquisition and possession of firearms, the European Firearms Card, the stripping of performance. The main demands in the document are about harmonising the marking of firearms and coordinating the use of various databases in order to facilitate the tracking of weapons and the fight against crime. On the other hand, the following fields of impact are taken into account in the development of the European Union's integrated firearms policy: the protection of the market for legal civilian firearms, the limitation of the entry of firearms into the hands of criminals, the increased pressure on criminal markets, the building of better intelligence capacity. Solutions that are included in the amended act. Records shall be entered concerning the type of alphabet and the minimum font dimensions of the marking of small arms and their essential parts. State-approved methods for the marking of small arms and their essential parts shall be laid down, with particular regard to the way in which the marking is applied to the frame or lock chamber of small firearms made of non-metallic materials, in particular polymer plastics. This includes firearms and signals by statutory regulations resulting from Directive 2019/69. Previously, this was not a regulated issue in Polish legislation. The law contains a whole package of rules that clarify the definition of signal and alarm weapons. The definition of ammunition has been clarified and extended by adding artillery ammunition and shell casings, mine hulls, propulsion systems components, missile guidance and control, air and deep-sea bombs, torpedoes and target imitators. Signal and alarm weapons and firearms that are devoid of use... Under Directive 2019/69, these devices may be exempted from the definition of firearms. The way in which the signal and alarm weapons have been manufactured is to ensure that these devices are in no way capable of projecting a missile and will only fulfil their primary task, i.e. to alarm, signal or save lives, and can only be used for these declared purposes. The categories and sub-categories of small arms are also to be included in transport consent forms and prior transport approvals in the case of arms movements within the European Union. Amendments to the term of validity of medical and psychological certificates issued for the purpose of granting or amending concessions have been proposed. The present law primarily implements EU law and, in the scope described above, contributes to greater transparency in the acquisition, manufacture and control of firearms, signals and alarms. In relation to the issue raised in the Act, mechanisms are created that enable the tracking and supervision of weapons and their sensitive components by giving them State-specific markings. The Left Club will support this bill, but it does make two amendments to it. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u331	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Gentlemen of Ministers! I have the honour to present on behalf of the committee a draft law amending the Act on the Safety of People in Water Areas. The Commission for Administration and Home Affairs examined the Act on 13 May 2021. The draft Act amending the Act on the Safety of Persons in Water Areas aims to clarify the legal provisions regarding the definition of water rescuer currently contained in the article. 2 point 5 of that law by specifying how many other qualifications useful in water rescue should be held by a water rescuer, as well as introducing a statutory authorisation for the Minister responsible for Home Affairs to issue a regulation setting out other qualifications useful in water rescue and a list of evidence of their possession. The draft clarifies that this is a useful qualification for water rescue as referred to in Article. 2 point 2 of that law, to carry out rescue activities listed in Article. 13 of this bill. The project also provides that persons performing medical professions: a doctor of the system of State Medical Rescue, a nurse of the system of State Medical Rescue, a paramedic, who carry out professional training, meet the requirements for having another qualification useful in water rescue. It was accepted that competence in the field of rescue medicine for persons performing medical professions would be exchanged at the level of the Act on other qualifications useful in water rescue. At present, the definition of a water rescuer does not indicate how many other skills a water rescuer should have. The planned regulation clarifies the requirements for the water rescuer by indicating that the water rescuer has at least one qualification useful in water rescue. The proposal therefore proposes to move the regulation concerning the definition of water rescuer to the new editorial unit and repeal point 5 of Article. 2 Act of 18 August 2011 on the safety of persons residing in water areas and after Article. 15 add Article. 15a. The draft law authorises the Minister responsible for Home Affairs to issue implementing rules specifying other qualifications useful in water rescue and a list of evidence of such qualifications, taking into account that these qualifications may be useful for rescue operations undertaken in the framework of water rescue. However, current legislation does not define the concept of other qualifications useful in water rescue, which makes it difficult to identify precisely which qualifications are included in the catalogue. The Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration has published guidelines in this area, but this issue should be regulated by law. The practice so far shows that other qualifications useful in water rescue are very important for professional water rescue. The absence of such qualifications of water rescuers will prevent the performance of basic statutory tasks by bodies authorised to perform water rescue. Designed regulation is expected in the environment of water rescuers. The bill does not generate administrative burdens, it will not result in additional costs for water rescuers. According to the current wording of the Act, water rescuers are obliged to have other qualifications useful in rescue. The proposal also provides for an authorisation for the Minister responsible for Home Affairs to issue a regulation, which will allow the creation of a catalogue of other qualifications useful in water rescue. Thus, the rule of law will specify precisely which qualifications allow for the acquisition of water lifeguard privileges. I would also like to stress that the bill is compatible with EU law. There was also an amendment tabled regarding the date of entry into force of the Act. The government side has accepted this amendment, the bill will not enter into force before the summer and summer season. The bill was proposed to enter into force on 1 October, which pleased the committee. The Committee on Administration and Home Affairs welcomes the draft law and recommends that it be adopted by the High House. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u333	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice in relation to the draft law amending the Act on the Safety of People in Water Areas from Print No 1126. Ladies and gentlemen! Many people in the water would like to take advantage of this opportunity to regenerate their strength, recover their health. The aquatic environment is such a primary environment for each of us, but unfortunately sometimes it can pose a fatal threat to us. It is therefore extremely important that emergency services operate in the water rescue sector. According to the World Health Organization, about 400,000 people die every year in the world as a result of drowning. persons. In Poland, data from the Police Headquarters show that this is how 500 to 700 people die each year. The sinking process is very fast and it is therefore extremely important that professional services act immediately here and now, unlike other services that can plan rescue operations. The Ministry has therefore prepared a draft amendment to the Act on the Safety of People in Water Areas. This project solves several problems that appeared in the Polish legal order. Firstly, the definition of a water lifeguard was currently missing. It was not indicated what other qualifications and how many of them should be provided by a water rescuer. There was a lack of regulation of the concept of other qualifications useful in water rescue. This draft law clarifies the documents confirming the acquisition of these other skills necessary for the operation of rescue operations. In the current legal order, a water rescuer may be a person who has knowledge and skills in the field of water rescue and swimming techniques, has other qualifications useful in water rescue, meets the requirements set out in the Act on State Medical Rescue for System Doctors, System Nurse, Medical Rescue Officer performing professional training and is employed or served in a craft authorised to perform water rescue. As you can see, at many moments, there is an expression that a lifeguard should have other qualifications useful when performing rescue operations. Rescue operations shall consist in particular of the acceptance of an accident or threat notification, the arrival of an accident with appropriate emergency equipment, the granting of qualified assistance, the security of the accident site, the evacuation of persons from a place which poses a risk to life and health and the transport of persons who have suffered an accident or are at risk of loss of life or health in the aquatic area. Therefore, other qualifications that are not currently defined in the legal order are necessary and should be useful for rescue operations listed in the above paragraphs 1. to 7. There is therefore an urgent need to regulate this situation. Today's draft law provides for the possibility of clarifying the other qualifications of a water rescuer, which will be necessary in carrying out these activities, as well as giving the Home Minister the power to define these qualifications by means of a regulation, an implementing act. And these are qualifications that can be useful in carrying out these tasks, e.g. qualifications for the management of vessels in inland waters and qualifications for water tourism, qualifications in maritime navigation, such as certificates, patents in inland navigation, motorboat rights, certificates and diplomas in maritime navigation. Qualification of persons who perform medical professions, here qualifications for confirmation of skills by a system doctor, nurse of State Medical Rescue or paramedic [[♪ The bell ♪]] It's a medical emergency. All these issues will be clarified in the draft law as well as in the implementing regulation, which is why it was necessary to prepare this amendment, for which I thank the Minister for Home Affairs and Administration very warmly. By the way, I would like to thank all the water rescuers who often devote their lives to these tasks so that we can rest safely over the water reservoirs. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u373	Thank you. Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club on the government draft law amending certain laws relating to benefits to the family, contained in print No 1015 and in print No 1148. High Izbo! The Government of Law and Justice, pursuing a policy of responsible development, is both undertaking a series of measures for a strong economy, as well as seeking a complementary and long-term family-friendly policy. We are introducing expected changes to reduce bureaucracy and make it easier for citizens and their offices to benefit from family benefits and maintenance funds. Key changes are therefore to be made to those who pay maintenance, maintenance debtors and all who take benefits from the maintenance fund. In 2023, there is to be a first valorisation of the income criterion entitled to benefit from the maintenance fund. An increase in the minimum wage for work results in a loss of entitlement to benefits for single parents and therefore the amount of the income criterion would be increased every 3 years by a valorisation rate. In addition, the National Debt Register will disclose both debtors for whom the execution of maintenance benefits is pending, as well as debtors for whom the execution of claims from the State budget is being carried out. A child's maintenance allowance not paid for at least 3 months or the ongoing execution of payments from the State budget will be sufficient to ensure that the person who refuses to pay is entered in the National Debt Register. He'll be in it as long as he pays his full debt. Amount of debts owed by maintenance debtors to the Treasury Countries grow by about PLN 1.5 billion each year. These debts are still subject to enforcement and should therefore remain in the Registers of Economic Information Offices until they are fully repaid so that knowledge of them is widely available as a sanction against debtors for failing to repay those debts. The contested law also provides for substantial changes to the Labour Code for employers who employ or pay them higher wages than the employment contracts do, without deductions on maintenance benefits. Currently, the employer learns that he employs a maintenance debtor, either from the debtor himself or from the bailiff's writings. After the amendment, the employer will be able to check the National Debt Register to see if he is dealing with a debtor. In conclusion, the contested bill relating to benefits to the family largely concerns the expected changes to the maintenance fund, which are intended to better secure families with children entitled to benefits from the fund, and introduces very severe sanctions for defaulting on maintenance benefits. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In view of the above, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of a government draft law amending certain laws relating to benefits to the family. Thank you. The aim of the support instruments was and is to minimise the negative effects of the pandemic and to return to economic normality as soon as possible. The introduced shields stabilise the Polish economy and give it an investment boost, and the aid instruments are constantly adapted to the needs of the environments that are most affected by the pandemic. By means of dialogue and in response to the needs of the next pandemic environments, the government has prepared an amendment to the Law on specific solutions related to the prevention, prevention and eradication of COVID-19 and other communicable diseases and the crisis situations caused by them and certain other laws contained in print Nos 1145 and 1145-A. The Act introduces several areas of change governing new rules concerning inter alia. the following issues: extension of the period for the use of the voucher for the implementation of the tourist event, extension of the deadline for the return of payments for tourism to the Tourist Return Fund. The World Pandemic prevented a large number of travellers from implementing accepted vouchers, and therefore it is appropriate to extend the period of use of these vouchers. Much of the vouchers concern winter and national tourism, including school and corporate trips. Vouchers will have an extended period of validity taking into account the existing rules established by tourism organisers with travellers. On the other hand, the extension of the deadline set for tourism to pay back to the Tourist Return Fund is justified by a prolonged epidemic. The introduction of the possibility to provide services in the repayment of support instruments received by entrepreneurs - this instrument responds to situations of chance which may prevent the fulfilment of commitments and the amendment introduces the possibility of redemption in whole or in part, deferment of payment deadlines, payment into instalments of the funds received under the support provided. Other tools are support for entrepreneurs engaged in the business of selling foodstuffs, paper, writing to students, listeners or pupils on the premises of educational systems. Subjects that have not been active throughout the closing period of schools or have been temporarily restricted will receive support under the sectoral shield. Other proposals for solutions in the bill include: changes due to the extension of time limits for registration of a vehicle, changes in the implementation of certain aid mechanisms, solutions for tenants operating in commercial galleries with a sales area of more than 2000 m2.
