ParlaMint-PL_2021-05-19-sejm-30-1.u485	Thank you very much. High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry and the Committee on Foreign Affairs, I present a report on the Government's draft Act on the ratification of the Minamata Convention on Mercury, drawn up at Kumamoto on 10 October 2013. The draft law contained in print No 1127 entered the Parliament on 26 April this year, and on 30 April it was addressed to first reading in committees. On 18 May, the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry and the Committee on Foreign Affairs carried out their first reading and then detailed work on the project, the result of which is the report. It was adopted by 45 votes in favour, with no votes against, with one abstention. High Izbo! The Minamata Convention on Mercury was concluded to protect human health and the environment from anthropogenic emissions and releases of mercury and mercury compounds. Ratification of the convention and obtaining the status of the parties by our country will allow Poland to participate in the conference of the parties, actively participate in decision-making, shaping guidelines and criteria in matters governed by the Convention. The Republic of Poland proposes here a balanced approach, i.e. avoiding unilateral actions which would result in a decrease in the competitiveness of the Polish economy and not have a global impact. In particular, the Convention covers the following issues: mercury supply sources and trade in mercury, as referred to in Article. 3 of the Convention - mercury-added products - this is dealt with by Article. 4 - production processes where mercury or its compounds are used - that is Article. 5 conventions - mining of traditional and small-scale gold, emissions of mercury and its compounds into the atmosphere - Article. 8 of the Convention - the release of mercury and its compounds into land and water - this is what Article treats. 9 - Transitional storage of mercury other than waste - that is included in Article. 10 of the Convention - the treatment of mercury waste and the identification and evaluation of places contaminated with mercury or its compounds - these are Article. Article 11 and Article 11 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council [2] shall apply mutatis mutandis. 12 conventions. High Izbo! Ratification of the Convention will not result in the need to amend the Polish legal order, except for amendments which result from the need to implement Regulation No 2017/852 of the European Parliament and of the Council of the European Union of 17 May 2017 on mercury. This regulation complements European law by bringing it into line with the Convention. I would like to point out that the European Union has also signed this convention. The 2017 European Union Regulation introduced the provisions of the Convention, which had not yet been included in the European Union acquis. On the other hand, Poland has rules on sources of supply of mercury, trade in mercury, mercury-containing products, emissions into the atmosphere, mercury waste and temporary storage resulting from both Polish law and the adaptation of EU law. Thus, a very small scope of rules on, in particular, the competent authority and the most important issue, i.e. the definition of a system of sanctions, remains to be completed. High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry and the Committee on Foreign Affairs, please adopt the Act on the ratification of the Minamata Convention on Mercury drawn up in Kumamoto on 10 October 2013 contained in the report of the Joint Committee on Printing No 1181. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The main objective of the Convention is to protect health and the environment from mercury-induced pollution and mercury-containing products. The Convention does not in itself prohibit mercury extraction if such extraction took place before the date of ratification of the Convention by the various parties. A literary award awarded by London's News from 1958 until the end of the 1980s. In spite of the formal closure of the magazine, the 20th century was the most prestigious literary distinction in emigration. The letter edited in London by Mieczysław Grydzewski, and after his death by Michał Chmielowiec and Stefania Kossowska, was the most important center integrating Polish emigration poets and writers, historians and critics of literature and art, regardless of political beliefs. It became a place of dialogue with world literature, a platform of Polish-Jewish agreement and a centre of free independence thought. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland hereby wishes to commemorate the 75th anniversary of the revival of 'News'. The Committee on Culture and Media requests the High Sejm to adopt this draft resolution. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

In consideration of the impact of the news, it is impossible to ignore the contribution made by the Literary News published in 1924 and 1939, which was edited by Mieczysław Grydzewski at the time. Initially, it was a literary newspaper with the advantage of information and column materials. Later, it contained mainly social, literary, cultural and political issues. In the 1930s, the weekly mainly represented liberal views. The letter did not have a political direction at the time, although after the coup in May it was strongly on the Sanitation side, but after the Brest Process it turned towards a democratic-liberal one. After the resumption of the activity on emigration, the letter continued its socio-cultural direction. Its importance for Polish culture is witnessed by such authors as: Słonimski, Tuwim, Irzykowski or Pruszyński in the Second Polish Republic, and from the times of emigration - Baliński, Hemar, Lechoń or Nowakowski. It also deserves attention granted from 1958 to 1990, i.e. nine years after the last issue, the award for the best Polish book published on emigration. The winners included such writers as Witold Gombrowicz, Czesław Miłosz and Marek Hłasko. The Parliamentary Club of the Polish Coalition and the Polish People's Party welcomes the initiative and thanks the Members of the applicant and the Library of the University. Nicolaus Copernicus in Toruń, because there are digitized, collected and organized collections of this publication in the Emigration Archive. Thank you very much. We will, of course, vote in favour.

According to the NIK report, one in 115 children is in the spectrum of autism. Not every one of these kids is alone. For many children with autism, it is necessary to point to permanent or long-term care or assistance of another person due to the significantly limited possibility of self-existence. It's so-called. point 7 of the Disability Decision. Usually, its indication means guaranteeing financial support, therapy or access to specialist doctors without a queue. At present, disability courts often receive paragraph 7 of the disability ruling by emancipating children. In this way, they ignore the advice of a doctor who knows the child best and is able to assess whether he needs to be cared for by another person because of his independence. The decision-making teams often decide to provide care only for children or people with disabilities, while autism is not visible. Autism is different from a typical way of human development, which reveals differences in communication, relationships, expression of emotions, learning and a diverse pattern of behavior. Some children will not be able to establish relationships with others, communicate their needs, express emotions without parental support. The introduction of an epidemic in the country has further complicated the situation. Disability groups may issue judgments without conducting a direct examination, which makes it even more difficult for people with a diagnosis of autism to carry out an objective assessment. Witold Pilecki is a legendary figure, his life was saturated with patriotism and heroism. He came from a noble family of independence traditions. In occupied Warsaw he co-created a secret Polish army. In order to gain German plans for concentration camps, he decided to enter the Auschwitz camp. He was the author of the first note on Nazi genocide. In the camp he created a Union of Military Organizations and a conspiracy network. In connection with the development of the camp resistance by the Nazis, Pilecki managed to escape from the camp in April 1943. He then actively participated in the war activities and in the Warsaw Uprising. On 8 May 1947, he was arrested by UB officers and then on 15 March 1948 - sentenced to death and executed. Bolesław Bierut refused his pardon. In the Polish People's Republic all information about it was subject to censorship. Hello and praise the heroes! Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The lack of regulation of land under buildings makes co-operatives deprived of the right to convert their cooperative rights into separate ownership. They are deprived of the possibility of establishing permanent books for their premises, and cannot sell their premises at market prices, as the value of these premises is about 20% lower than similar premises on already regulated land. In addition, such persons have housing but do not have the quality of the party in administrative proceedings. Unfortunately, local authorities are often not able to regulate the land for housing cooperatives and co-operatives, because the current legal situation is optimal for local governments. For example, the Warsaw government can collect money from the cooperative for the uncontracting use of the land, and the cooperative, in turn, transfers fees to residents. It is not on hand to regulate the land, because co-operatives could then convert perpetual use into property. The case should have been settled a long time ago, but unfortunately the local authorities do not want to do so. For this reason, cases often go to courts, where, for example, The Warsaw government requests that the cases be dismissed due to the statute of limitations, although the limitation itself has led to it. Unfortunately, the lack of a willingness to regulate land for the benefit of the co-operatives by local authorities causes the inhabitants of buildings to suffer mostly. This also means that there will be a need to amend the law, which will lead to land being owned by co-operatives under the law, without looking at large cities. Thank you very much.
